    3683             :        *
    3684             :        * Subdivision of a NURB curve can be effected by adding a
    3685             :        * control point (insertion of a knot), or by raising the
    3686             :        * degree of the functions used to build the NURB. The
    3687             :        * expression
    3688             :        *
    3689             :        *     `degree = knots - controlpoints + 1` (J Walter piece)
    3690             :        *     `degree = knots - controlpoints` (Blender implementation)
    3691             :        *       ( this is confusing.... what is true? Another concern
    3692             :        *       is that the JW piece allows the curve to become
    3693             :        *       explicitly 1st order derivative discontinuous, while
    3694             :        *       this is not what we want here... )
    3695             :        *
    3696             :        * is an invariant for a single NURB curve. Raising the degree
    3697             :        * of the NURB is done elsewhere; the degree is assumed

    3700             :        * per control point - this can be misleading).
    3701             :        * Adding a knot is done by searching for the place in the

    3703             :        * by picking an appropriate knot value between two existing
    3704             :        * ones. The number of control-points that is influenced by the
    3705             :        * insertion depends on the order of the curve. A certain
    3706             :        * minimum number of knots is needed to form high-order
    3707             :        * curves, as can be seen from the equation above. In Blender,
    3708             :        * currently NURBs may be up to 6th order, so we modify at
    3709             :        * most 6 points. One point is added. For an n-degree curve,
    3710             :        * n points are discarded, and n+1 points inserted
    3711             :        * (so effectively, n points are modified).  (that holds for
    3712             :        * the JW piece, but it seems not for our NURBs)
    3713             :        * In practice, the knot spacing is copied, but the tail
    3714             :        * (the points following the insertion point) need to be
    3715             :        * offset to keep the knot series ascending. The knot series
